Web app wrapper that makes a native web app look mobile for Android and ios
ChatGPT said:

Thought for 15s
If you already have a web app and want it to ship as an Android + iOS “mobile app” (home-screen icon, splash screen, App/Play Store distribution, full-screen, native-feeling chrome), you generally have two layers to choose from:

A wrapper/packager (turns your web app into an installable app)

A mobile UI layer (makes the web UI look/behave like a mobile app)

Below are the best-practice options.

1) Best “wrapper” options (Android + iOS)
A) Capacitor (recommended for most web apps)
What it is: A modern WebView wrapper that packages your web app into native shells, with optional native plugins (camera, push, deep links, etc.).
Best for: You want minimum rewrite, keep your web stack, but still need store distribution + native capabilities.

Pros

Very strong “web-first” workflow

Good plugin ecosystem and native bridge

Easier than older Cordova-style setups for many teams

Cons

Still a WebView app (performance depends on web code)

Some “native polish” features require extra work/plugins

Typical stacks: React/Vue/Angular/Svelte + Capacitor (often via Ionic, but not required)

B) React Native or Flutter + WebView (when you need hybrid UI)
What it is: A native app framework where you embed your web app inside a WebView, and optionally build some screens natively.
Best for: You want the web app embedded, but also want true native UI for critical screens (navigation, settings, onboarding, etc.).

Pros

Can mix native UI + web screens

Better path if you’ll gradually migrate to native later

Cons

More engineering overhead than Capacitor

Two paradigms (native app + web app) to maintain

C) PWA (no wrapper; install from browser)
What it is: Progressive Web App installed from the browser; can be full-screen with an icon and offline caching.
Best for: You want fastest path and don’t strictly need App Store distribution.

Pros

One build, simplest maintenance

Great for Android, improving on iOS over time

Cons

iOS limitations still exist (background behavior, some APIs)

App Store presence is not the same as a packaged app

D) Trusted Web Activity (Android-only)
What it is: Android technique that runs your PWA in a full-screen Chrome instance with no browser UI.
Best for: Android store distribution for a PWA with excellent performance and fewer WebView quirks.

Pros

Strong Android experience for PWAs

Good security model

Cons

Android-only (you still need something else for iOS)

E) Cordova (legacy)
Still used, but generally considered older tech compared to Capacitor for new builds.

2) If you mean “make my web UI look like a mobile app”
A wrapper alone won’t make a desktop-style web UI feel mobile. You typically also apply one of:

A) Ionic UI (mobile-style components for web)
Mobile navigation patterns, tabs, sheets, modals, iOS/Android look-and-feel

Works great with Capacitor

B) Framework7
Strong “native mobile UI” vibe for web apps (iOS-like especially)

C) Tailwind + a mobile design system
If you want custom design but mobile-first layout and touch ergonomics

Key UI items to implement regardless:

Proper viewport meta, safe-area padding (iPhone notch)

Touch-sized controls (44px+ targets), bottom nav, gesture-friendly modals

Splash screen + status bar handling (wrapper config)

Offline/loading states and fast startup

Practical recommendation (most common winning path)
If your goal is: “Take my existing web app and publish to iOS + Android with minimal rewrite”:

Go with:

Capacitor as the wrapper

Add Ionic UI (optional) if you want instant mobile-native components

Ensure your web app is responsive/mobile-first (this is non-negotiable for “native feel”)

If you tell me:

your current stack (React/Vue/Angular/Next.js/etc.)

whether you must be in App Store/Play Store

what native features you need (push, camera, biometrics, background tasks)

…I can give you a concrete, shortest-path implementation plan for your stack.

ChatGPT said:

Thought for 16s
If you want a TypeScript-first way to wrap an existing web app into a “native-feeling” Android + iOS app, the most direct path is Capacitor. Below is a practical, copy/paste setup (TypeScript config included), plus an alternative React Native WebView wrapper (also TypeScript) if you prefer a more “native shell” approach.

Option A (recommended): Capacitor wrapper (TypeScript)
1) Install Capacitor
npm i @capacitor/core @capacitor/cli
npx cap init
Capacitor will ask:

App name (e.g., TradeCentral)

App ID (e.g., com.yourco.tradecentral)

2) Ensure your web build outputs to a folder (example: dist)
If you use Vite (common in TS apps), it already builds to dist.

Build your web app:

npm run build
3) Create capacitor.config.ts
Place this at your project root:

import type { CapacitorConfig } from '@capacitor/cli';

const config: CapacitorConfig = {
  appId: 'com.yourco.yourapp',
  appName: 'YourApp',
  webDir: 'dist',
  bundledWebRuntime: false,

  // Optional: if you need local dev server access from device/simulator
  server: {
    androidScheme: 'https',
  },
};

export default config;
4) Add Android + iOS platforms
npm i @capacitor/android @capacitor/ios
npx cap add android
npx cap add ios
Sync web build into native projects:

npm run build
npx cap sync
Open in IDEs:

npx cap open android
npx cap open ios
iOS builds require macOS + Xcode.

5) Helpful package.json scripts
{
  "scripts": {
    "build": "vite build",
    "cap:sync": "npm run build && npx cap sync",
    "cap:android": "npm run cap:sync && npx cap open android",
    "cap:ios": "npm run cap:sync && npx cap open ios"
  }
}
Option A1: Capacitor wrapping a hosted web app (no bundling)
If your web app is already deployed (e.g., https://app.yoursite.com) and you want the native shell to load that URL:

import type { CapacitorConfig } from '@capacitor/cli';

const config: CapacitorConfig = {
  appId: 'com.yourco.yourapp',
  appName: 'YourApp',
  webDir: 'dist', // still required, can be minimal
  server: {
    url: 'https://app.yoursite.com',
    cleartext: false
  }
};

export default config;
This is fast, but your app becomes dependent on network availability unless you add offline strategy.

Mobile “native feel” checklist (inside your TypeScript web app)
1) Add viewport (in index.html)
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1, viewport-fit=cover" />
2) Safe-area + touch-friendly CSS
:root {
  --safe-top: env(safe-area-inset-top);
  --safe-right: env(safe-area-inset-right);
  --safe-bottom: env(safe-area-inset-bottom);
  --safe-left: env(safe-area-inset-left);
}

body {
  padding-top: var(--safe-top);
  padding-right: var(--safe-right);
  padding-bottom: var(--safe-bottom);
  padding-left: var(--safe-left);

  -webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent;
  -webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%;
  overscroll-behavior: none;
}

button, a, input, select {
  touch-action: manipulation;
}
3) (Optional) Detect Capacitor at runtime (TypeScript)
export function isNativeShell(): boolean {
  return !!(window as any).Capacitor?.isNativePlatform?.();
}
Option B: React Native WebView wrapper (TypeScript)
If you want a “native container” app that just displays your web app (and you can later add native screens), use React Native + WebView.

1) Core screen (TypeScript)
// App.tsx
import React from 'react';
import { SafeAreaView, StatusBar, Platform } from 'react-native';
import { WebView } from 'react-native-webview';

const WEB_URL = 'https://app.yoursite.com';

export default function App() {
  return (
    <SafeAreaView style={{ flex: 1 }}>
      <StatusBar barStyle={Platform.OS === 'ios' ? 'dark-content' : 'default'} />
      <WebView
        source={{ uri: WEB_URL }}
        javaScriptEnabled
        domStorageEnabled
        originWhitelist={['*']}
        allowsInlineMediaPlayback
        mediaPlaybackRequiresUserAction={false}
      />
    </SafeAreaView>
  );
}
This is heavier than Capacitor, but gives you more room to “go native” over time.

ChatGPT said:

Thought for 45s
You can absolutely build a native (Android + iOS) app while keeping a single system of truth—but the critical architectural decision is this:

The backend trading platform (your server + DB + engine) must remain the canonical source of truth.
The mobile app is a client that renders state, submits intents, and optionally caches for performance/offline, but it must not become a second “truth” that can diverge.

Two viable paths—pick based on how “native” you really need to be.

1) Fastest path to “native app feel” while keeping 1 truth: Capacitor wrapper
If you wrap the existing TradingCentral web app using Capacitor, you get:

Android/iOS installable app

Home-screen icon, splash, full-screen

Access to native APIs via plugins if needed (push, biometrics, etc.)

Zero divergence risk, because it is literally the same UI hitting the same API/DB/engine

Single source of truth is automatic because:

The app uses the same API calls and WebSocket streaming as the web client

No separate mobile data model is required

This is the best path if you want mobile distribution quickly and you already have a working web UX.

2) True native UI while keeping 1 truth: Native client + API-first platform
If you want a real native UI (React Native / Flutter / Kotlin+Swift), you must treat TradingCentral as an API platform:

Non-negotiables for “single system of truth”
All writes go through the API (orders, cancels, SL/TP edits, profile settings, risk settings).

Risk, margin, fills, equity, and audit events are computed server-side (engine-driven), never trusted from mobile.

Mobile maintains a cache, not a database-of-record.

Recommended architecture for TradingCentral: “API + Realtime + Sync”
A) Three layers of data
1) Authoritative state (server + DB)
Accounts, balances, margin, equity

Positions, orders, trades

Risk limits and enforcement decisions

Audit trail / order intent audit

2) Realtime streaming (WebSocket)
Quotes

Account snapshot updates (equity/free margin)

Position changes

Order lifecycle events (RECEIVED → ACCEPTED/REJECTED → FILLED/CANCELLED)

Trade audit events

3) Local cache (mobile-only, optional)
Used for:

Fast rendering

Offline UI continuity

Minimal offline actions (queue intents carefully)

Not used as the platform truth.

B) The “single source of truth” rule for mobile
Reads
Initial load: REST “snapshot” endpoints

Live updates: WebSocket subscription

Background refresh: periodic REST reconcile

Writes
Submit intents only: “place order”, “cancel”, “update SL/TP”, etc.

Server responds with an ack + correlationId

Final outcome arrives via WS (“order accepted/filled/rejected”)

This prevents mobile from ever “assuming” an order happened.

Concrete implementation blueprint (TypeScript-first)
Even without the ZIP in this chat session, here is the structure that consistently avoids desync.

1) Define a shared contract package (TypeScript)
Create a shared module used by server, web, and mobile:

shared/contracts/* (Zod schemas + inferred types)

shared/api-client/* (typed client, request helpers)

Example: shared/contracts/orders.ts

import { z } from "zod";

export const PlaceOrderRequest = z.object({
  symbol: z.string(),
  side: z.enum(["BUY", "SELL"]),
  type: z.enum(["MARKET", "LIMIT", "STOP"]),
  qty: z.number().positive(),
  price: z.number().optional(),         // required for LIMIT/STOP
  sl: z.number().optional(),
  tp: z.number().optional(),
  clientRequestId: z.string().min(10),  // idempotency key from client
});

export type PlaceOrderRequest = z.infer<typeof PlaceOrderRequest>;

export const PlaceOrderAck = z.object({
  correlationId: z.string(),
  status: z.enum(["RECEIVED"]),
  receivedAt: z.string(),              // ISO
});

export type PlaceOrderAck = z.infer<typeof PlaceOrderAck>;
Why this matters: it ensures the web app and native app can never silently drift on request/response shapes.

2) Idempotency + correlation IDs on all write endpoints
For any “write” call, enforce:

clientRequestId (idempotency key)

correlationId assigned server-side (ties audit + lifecycle events)

Outcome: retries on flaky mobile networks do not duplicate orders.

3) WebSocket “event bus” for unified live updates
Use a single WS endpoint (e.g., /ws) and multiplex channels:

quotes:<symbol>

account:<userId>

positions:<userId>

orders:<userId>

audit:<userId> (or admin channels)

Events must be append-only style (immutable facts). Example:

export type WsEvent =
  | { t: "QUOTE"; symbol: string; bid: number; ask: number; ts: number }
  | { t: "ACCOUNT_SNAPSHOT"; equity: number; freeMargin: number; ts: number }
  | { t: "ORDER_EVENT"; correlationId: string; stage: "ACCEPTED" | "REJECTED" | "FILLED" | "CANCELLED"; reason?: string; ts: number }
  | { t: "POSITION_EVENT"; positionId: string; action: "OPEN" | "UPDATE" | "CLOSE"; ts: number };
Single source of truth is preserved because the server emits the authoritative events.

4) Mobile state model: “Server cache with reconcile”
In the mobile app:

Keep a local store of the last known server snapshot (SQLite/MMKV/AsyncStorage depending on volume)

Apply WS events to update the store

Periodically reconcile by calling REST snapshots (especially after reconnect)

Best practice: “Snapshot + delta cursor”
Add a /sync endpoint:

Client stores cursor

Server returns “changes since cursor” + new cursor

This solves:

missed WS events

app backgrounding

intermittent connectivity

5) Offline mode (optional, but do it safely)
Offline trading is dangerous in market systems. If you support it at all:

Allow only “draft orders” offline (not submitted)

Or allow queued intents with strict gating:

only for demo trading

only with “stale quote” warnings

submit when online, but server still validates everything

If this is a hedge-fund-grade system, the safe posture is:
offline viewing yes; offline execution no (or demo-only).

Choosing between Wrapper vs True Native (practical guidance)
Choose Capacitor wrapper if:
You want iOS/Android apps quickly

Your web UI is already close to mobile-friendly

You want lowest risk of divergence

You want to keep one codebase now

Choose True Native if:
You need native navigation/gestures/performance that web can’t match

You plan to invest in a long-term mobile product

You need deeper native integrations (background tasks, advanced push workflows, etc.)

Hybrid migration strategy (often the best)
Ship Capacitor first (immediate distribution, same system of truth)

Add native features via Capacitor plugins as needed

If you still want full native later, build a native app that consumes the same API/WS contracts you already hardened

Executive recommendation
Phase 0 (fastest, lowest risk): Capacitor wrapper
Packages your existing web app into iOS/Android shells.

Uses the same UI and the same API/WS, so “single source of truth” stays intact by default.

This is the quickest path to “native presence” while you harden realtime + sync.

Phase 1 (required for hedge-fund-grade correctness): API + Realtime + Sync contract
Whether you wrap or go true-native, you must standardize:

Versioned REST API for snapshots and writes

WebSocket event stream for deltas

Idempotent writes + correlation IDs for order lifecycle

Cursor-based sync to recover from disconnect/backgrounding

Phase 2 (true native UI): React Native / Flutter consuming the same contracts
Mobile becomes a typed API client with local cache.

The backend remains the only authoritative state.

Single system of truth: the rule-set
1) Backend is authoritative
Balances, equity, margin, fills, positions, risk decisions, audit events: server/engine computed.

Mobile/web never “compute final truth” beyond UI display.

2) Clients submit intents, not outcomes
Place order, cancel, modify SL/TP, etc. are requests.

Server responds immediately with an ACK (RECEIVED) and correlationId.

Final result arrives via WS events: ACCEPTED/REJECTED/FILLED/CANCELLED.

3) Clients reconcile
Clients load a REST snapshot on startup.

Apply WS deltas.

If WS disconnects or app backgrounds, client calls /sync?cursor=... to catch up.

This guarantees convergence to one truth.

Concrete platform blueprint (TypeScript)
A) Shared contracts (used by server + web + mobile)
Create shared/contracts/* with Zod schemas + inferred types.

// shared/contracts/ws.ts
import { z } from "zod";

export const WsEvent = z.discriminatedUnion("t", [
  z.object({ t: z.literal("QUOTE"), symbol: z.string(), bid: z.number(), ask: z.number(), ts: z.number() }),
  z.object({ t: z.literal("ACCOUNT_SNAPSHOT"), equity: z.number(), freeMargin: z.number(), balance: z.number(), ts: z.number() }),
  z.object({
    t: z.literal("ORDER_EVENT"),
    correlationId: z.string(),
    stage: z.enum(["RECEIVED", "ACCEPTED", "REJECTED", "FILLED", "CANCELLED"]),
    reason: z.string().optional(),
    ts: z.number(),
  }),
  z.object({
    t: z.literal("POSITION_EVENT"),
    positionId: z.string(),
    action: z.enum(["OPEN", "UPDATE", "CLOSE"]),
    symbol: z.string(),
    ts: z.number(),
  }),
]);

export type WsEvent = z.infer<typeof WsEvent>;
// shared/contracts/orders.ts
import { z } from "zod";

export const PlaceOrderRequest = z.object({
  symbol: z.string(),
  side: z.enum(["BUY", "SELL"]),
  type: z.enum(["MARKET", "LIMIT", "STOP"]),
  qty: z.number().positive(),
  price: z.number().optional(),          // required for LIMIT/STOP
  sl: z.number().optional(),
  tp: z.number().optional(),
  clientRequestId: z.string().min(16),   // idempotency key
});

export const PlaceOrderAck = z.object({
  correlationId: z.string(),
  status: z.literal("RECEIVED"),
  receivedAt: z.string(),               // ISO
});

export type PlaceOrderRequest = z.infer<typeof PlaceOrderRequest>;
export type PlaceOrderAck = z.infer<typeof PlaceOrderAck>;
Why this matters: both web and native app compile against the same types, so you cannot drift.

B) Idempotency and correlation IDs on every write
On the server, enforce uniqueness of (userId, clientRequestId).

Schema pattern (SQLite/Drizzle conceptually):

order_intent_audit: userId, clientRequestId, correlationId, receivedAt, requestPayloadHash, decision, rejectReason, etc.

Unique index: (userId, clientRequestId).

Server handler sketch:

// server/routes/orders.ts
import { PlaceOrderRequest, PlaceOrderAck } from "../../shared/contracts/orders";
import { randomUUID } from "crypto";

export async function placeOrder(req: any, res: any) {
  const parsed = PlaceOrderRequest.safeParse(req.body);
  if (!parsed.success) return res.status(400).json({ error: "INVALID_REQUEST", details: parsed.error.flatten() });

  const userId = req.user.id;
  const { clientRequestId } = parsed.data;

  // 1) check idempotency
  const existing = await db.query.orderIntentAudit.findFirst({
    where: (t: any, { and, eq }: any) => and(eq(t.userId, userId), eq(t.clientRequestId, clientRequestId)),
  });

  if (existing) {
    // return same ack (idempotent)
    const ack = PlaceOrderAck.parse({
      correlationId: existing.correlationId,
      status: "RECEIVED",
      receivedAt: existing.receivedAt,
    });
    return res.json(ack);
  }

  // 2) create correlation id
  const correlationId = randomUUID();

  // 3) persist ORDER_RECEIVED in order_intent_audit
  await db.insert(orderIntentAudit).values({
    userId,
    clientRequestId,
    correlationId,
    receivedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    decision: "RECEIVED",
    // store minimal request fields (and/or hash the full payload)
    symbol: parsed.data.symbol,
    side: parsed.data.side,
    type: parsed.data.type,
    qty: parsed.data.qty,
    price: parsed.data.price ?? null,
    sl: parsed.data.sl ?? null,
    tp: parsed.data.tp ?? null,
  });

  // 4) enqueue to engine (engine decides ACCEPT/REJECT/FILL etc.)
  await engine.enqueueOrder({ userId, correlationId, ...parsed.data });

  // 5) respond ack immediately
  return res.json(PlaceOrderAck.parse({
    correlationId,
    status: "RECEIVED",
    receivedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
  }));
}
This is what prevents duplicate order placement on flaky mobile connections.

C) WebSocket as the unified live-update channel
You want one server WS endpoint (e.g., /ws) that publishes:

quote updates

account snapshots

order lifecycle events

position lifecycle events

audit events (admin)

Server-side publish abstraction:

// server/realtime/bus.ts
type Client = { send: (s: string) => void; userId: number };
const clients = new Set<Client>();

export function registerClient(c: Client) { clients.add(c); }
export function unregisterClient(c: Client) { clients.delete(c); }

export function publishToUser(userId: number, event: unknown) {
  const msg = JSON.stringify(event);
  for (const c of clients) if (c.userId === userId) c.send(msg);
}
Engine uses it:

// server/engine/events.ts
import { publishToUser } from "../realtime/bus";

export function emitOrderStage(userId: number, correlationId: string, stage: string, reason?: string) {
  publishToUser(userId, { t: "ORDER_EVENT", correlationId, stage, reason, ts: Date.now() });
}
D) Cursor-based sync endpoint (the missing piece most apps forget)
WebSockets are not enough by themselves because mobile apps:

background

lose connectivity

miss events

Add a /api/v1/sync?cursor=... endpoint that returns events since cursor.

Minimal pattern:

Every event persisted with monotonic seq (auto-increment).

Client stores last applied seq.

// shared/contracts/sync.ts
import { z } from "zod";
import { WsEvent } from "./ws";

export const SyncResponse = z.object({
  nextCursor: z.number(),
  events: z.array(WsEvent),
});

export type SyncResponse = z.infer<typeof SyncResponse>;
Server:

// server/routes/sync.ts
import { SyncResponse } from "../../shared/contracts/sync";

export async function sync(req: any, res: any) {
  const userId = req.user.id;
  const cursor = Number(req.query.cursor ?? 0) || 0;

  const rows = await db.query.eventLog.findMany({
    where: (t: any, { and, eq, gt }: any) => and(eq(t.userId, userId), gt(t.seq, cursor)),
    orderBy: (t: any, { asc }: any) => asc(t.seq),
    limit: 2000,
  });

  const events = rows.map((r: any) => JSON.parse(r.payloadJson));
  const nextCursor = rows.length ? rows[rows.length - 1].seq : cursor;

  return res.json(SyncResponse.parse({ nextCursor, events }));
}
Mobile startup flow:

GET /snapshot

open WS

GET /sync?cursor=lastCursor after reconnect/background

This makes “single truth” robust.

Native app options while retaining synced data
Option 1: Capacitor (recommended first)
Mobile app is your existing TradingCentral React app packaged.

Uses your current auth/session strategy.

Uses the same WS layer you’re already considering (“LiveUpdatesProvider”).

Outcome: zero duplicated business logic, minimal engineering risk.

Option 2: React Native (TypeScript) true native UI
Use a shared TS client library (shared/api-client) for REST + WS + sync.

Persist cache in SQLite (expo-sqlite) or MMKV depending on needs.

Keep all computations server-driven.

Outcome: best native UX; higher build cost; still one truth if you follow the rule-set.

“Single system of truth” implementation checklist (what you must enforce)
No client-derived balances/margin: computed server-side only.

Order idempotency (clientRequestId) on every write endpoint.

Correlation ID per order attempt, used in audit tables + WS lifecycle events.

Event log + cursor sync to recover missed WS deltas.

Authoritative snapshots: account/positions/orders snapshot endpoints exist and are fast.

Engine emits events; UI never “assumes fill.”

Version your API (/api/v1) to support mobile releases.

Security: token/session stored securely on mobile (Keychain/Keystore).

Backpressure: limit WS subscriptions; send per-user topics; compress if needed.

Thought for 6m 34s
I reviewed the TradingCentral-3AL-V3 ZIP you referenced. You already have the core primitives needed to ship Android/iOS while keeping one system of truth:

Backend (authoritative): Express + SQLite + trading logic (order engine + recalcAccount) in /server.

REST API: /api/... endpoints used by the web client.

Realtime: a WebSocket server on /ws (in server/routes.ts) that currently broadcasts lightweight signals:

{ type: "quotes:updated" }

{ type: "trades:updated", userId }

Web client: React (Vite) + React Query. It already listens to /ws and uses those signals to invalidate REST queries:

client/src/hooks/use-quotes.tsx invalidates "/api/quotes/latest"

client/src/hooks/use-trades.tsx invalidates "/api/trades", "/api/trades/open", "/api/account/summary", "/api/auth/current-user"

This is a good base. The main architectural rule to preserve “single source of truth” is:

All balances, margin, equity, fills, and trade state are computed server-side.
Clients (web/mobile) only render snapshots + apply server-emitted deltas (or refetch when signaled).

Recommended mobile path (fastest, least divergence): Capacitor wrapper
Why this fits your repo
Your client code uses relative URLs (/api/..., /ws) with cookie sessions (credentials: "include" in client/src/lib/queryClient.ts). That works best when the UI is served from the same origin as the API.

So the cleanest approach is: Capacitor loads your deployed TradingCentral web app URL (served by your Express server), and your existing /api + /ws continue to work unchanged.

Capacitor config tailored to your repo
Your Vite build output is dist/public (vite.config.ts), so your Capacitor webDir must reflect that.

// capacitor.config.ts (repo root)
import type { CapacitorConfig } from "@capacitor/cli";

const config: CapacitorConfig = {
  appId: "com.yourco.tradingcentral",
  appName: "TradingCentral",
  webDir: "dist/public",

  // Recommended for “single origin” + cookie sessions:
  // Load the app from your deployed server so /api and /ws stay same-origin.
  server: {
    url: "https://YOUR_DEPLOYED_DOMAIN",   // e.g. https://tradecentral.yourdomain.com
    cleartext: false,
    androidScheme: "https",
  },
};

export default config;

Why this preserves the single system of truth
The “app” is the same React UI you already ship.

All reads/writes still go to the same backend and database.

Realtime continues through /ws.

No duplicated business logic appears in mobile.

This is the correct Phase 0 for speed + correctness.

True native UI (React Native/Flutter) without losing sync
If you want a genuinely native UI (not a WebView shell), the rule-set becomes stricter:

Data contract
Startup snapshot via REST

/api/auth/current-user

/api/account/summary

/api/trades, /api/trades/open

/api/quotes/latest

Live updates via WebSocket

Connect to /ws

Authenticate ({ type: "auth", userId }), matching your current server logic

Client is a cache, not a truth

Store last snapshot locally (SQLite/MMKV/etc.) only for UX/perf.

Never compute authoritative margin/equity locally.

Auth reality check (important)
Right now you rely on cookie sessions. In true-native, cookie handling across REST + WebSocket is doable but more fragile than web.

Two viable approaches:

Keep cookie sessions and use a native cookie manager (more moving parts).

Add a token auth mode for mobile (recommended long-term): login returns access token; WS auth uses token.

If you’re shipping Capacitor first, you can defer token auth until you commit to true-native.

What you should tighten in this repo to make sync “mobile-grade”
1) Add an account/equity realtime signal (fixes your “needs refresh” symptoms)
You already recalc accounts on quote updates (in server/feeds/forgeFeed.ts you call recalcAccount(userId) for affected users). But your client only refetches account summary on trades:updated, not on quotes:updated.

Net effect: equity/free margin can lag until a refresh or a trade event, even though the backend updated it.

Minimal client-side fix (immediate): also invalidate account summary when quotes update.

// client/src/hooks/use-quotes.tsx (inside onMessage)
if (message.type === "quotes:updated") {
  queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["/api/quotes/latest"] });
  queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["/api/account/summary"] });
  queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["/api/auth/current-user"] });
}
Better server-side fix (scalable): broadcast { type: "account:updated", userId } only to the affected user(s), whenever recalcAccount(userId) completes. Then the client invalidates only when it needs to.

That becomes critical when you move to mobile + many concurrent users.

2) One WS connection (your scalability objective)
Right now each hook opens its own WebSocket (use-quotes and use-trades both connect to /ws). You explicitly want a single LiveUpdatesProvider to own one connection and fan out updates.

This is the right move for:

mobile battery usage

server connection counts

avoiding duplicate reconnect storms

3) Add idempotency keys for mobile writes (prevents duplicate orders on flaky networks)
Your audit system has correlation IDs, but your write endpoints don’t enforce idempotency by a client-provided key. On mobile, retries happen often.

Practical pattern:

client sends clientRequestId (UUID) on place/close/modify

server stores it (unique per user) and returns the same ACK if repeated

This is one of the most important “single truth” protections for real mobile usage.

Practical decision
If your priority is “ship mobile fast, no divergence risk”:

Do Capacitor remote URL first (same origin, same API, same WS, same auth).

If your priority is “native UI + long-term mobile product”:

Still ship Capacitor first (it de-risks backend contracts),

then build a true-native app against the same REST+WS contracts once you’ve:

unified WS into one provider

added account update events

added idempotency keys

If you want, I can produce the exact repo-aligned implementation for:

LiveUpdatesProvider (single WS connection) + refactors of use-quotes / use-trades

server-side account:updated broadcast pattern (so equity/free margin never requires refresh)

an API base/WS base abstraction so Capacitor can run either remote URL or bundled assets cleanly without breaking /api calls.

Thought for 14m 39s
Below is a refactor blueprint that turns TradingCentral into an API-first + WebSocket-first platform (single source of truth = server DB + engine), so you can ship Capacitor immediately and evolve to true native long-run without reworking core trading logic.

The key change is: all state flows through REST snapshots + WS deltas, and all producers (ForgeFeed, order engine, manual trade routes, account recalcs) publish into one realtime bus.

Target architecture (what we are enforcing)
1) Canonical truth
Authoritative state: SQLite trading_app.db + engine decisions.

Clients (web/capacitor/native): render state, submit intents, maintain cache, never compute final balances/margins/fills.

2) Read model
REST = snapshots (/api/account/summary, /api/trades, /api/quotes/latest, etc.)

WS = deltas (quote ticks, trades changed, account snapshot changed)

REST /api/v1/sync?cursor= = catch-up after backgrounding/reconnect (native-critical)

3) Write model
REST = intents (place/cancel/close/modify), engine updates DB, publishes WS events.

Implementation: add a realtime bus + attach producers + single client connection
Everything below is inline, ready-to-drop, using your repo’s actual structure.

1) NEW: shared/realtime.ts (contracts you will reuse for native later)
// shared/realtime.ts
import { z } from "zod";

/**
 * Server -> Client events
 * Keep "type" strings compatible with existing client hooks (quotes:updated / trades:updated)
 * while adding richer payloads for native.
 */
export const RealtimeServerEvent = z.discriminatedUnion("type", [
  z.object({
    type: z.literal("hello"),
    serverTime: z.number(),
    cursor: z.number(),
    userId: z.number().nullable(),
  }),

  // High-frequency quote tick (WS only; not required to persist)
  z.object({
    type: z.literal("quotes:tick"),
    symbol: z.string(),
    name: z.string().optional(),
    price: z.number(),
    change: z.number().optional(),
    time: z.number().optional(),
    bid: z.number().optional(),
    ask: z.number().optional(),
    spread: z.number().optional(),
    prevClose: z.number().optional(),
    changePct: z.number().optional(),
    percent_change: z.number().optional(),
    isStale: z.boolean().optional(),
    lastApiUpdate: z.number().optional(),
    dataAge: z.number().optional(),
  }),

  // Low-frequency “revision” marker (safe to persist)
  z.object({
    type: z.literal("quotes:updated"),
    cursor: z.number().optional(),
    rev: z.number().optional(),
    symbols: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
    ts: z.number().optional(),
  }),

  z.object({
    type: z.literal("trades:updated"),
    cursor: z.number().optional(),
    userId: z.number().optional(),
    ts: z.number().optional(),
  }),

  z.object({
    type: z.literal("account:updated"),
    cursor: z.number().optional(),
    userId: z.number(),
    balance: z.number(),
    equity: z.number(),
    floatingPnl: z.number(),
    usedMargin: z.number(),
    freeMargin: z.number(),
    marginLevel: z.number().nullable(),
    openPositions: z.number(),
    pricingStale: z.boolean(),
    staleSymbols: z.array(z.string()),
    asOf: z.string(), // ISO
  }),
]);

export type RealtimeServerEvent = z.infer<typeof RealtimeServerEvent>;

/**
 * Client -> Server messages
 */
export const RealtimeClientMsg = z.discriminatedUnion("type", [
  // Backwards-compatible (older client code sends {type:"auth", userId})
  z.object({
    type: z.literal("auth"),
    userId: z.number().optional(),
    token: z.string().optional(), // reserved for future native JWT/opaque token auth
  }),

  // Subscribe model (native-friendly)
  z.object({
    type: z.literal("subscribe"),
    quotes: z.union([z.literal("ALL"), z.array(z.string())]).optional(),
    account: z.boolean().optional(),
    trades: z.boolean().optional(),
  }),

  z.object({
    type: z.literal("unsubscribe"),
    quotes: z.union([z.literal("ALL"), z.array(z.string())]).optional(),
    account: z.boolean().optional(),
    trades: z.boolean().optional(),
  }),

  z.object({ type: z.literal("ping") }),
]);

export type RealtimeClientMsg = z.infer<typeof RealtimeClientMsg>;
2) NEW: server/realtime/liveUpdates.ts (the WS engine + event log + publish API)
This is the single realtime “truth pipe”. Producers call liveUpdates.publish...().

// server/realtime/liveUpdates.ts
// @ts-nocheck
import Database from "better-sqlite3";
import type { WebSocketServer } from "ws";
import type session from "express-session";

type ClientState = {
  userId: number | null;
  quotesAll: boolean;
  quoteSymbols: Set<string>;
  wantsTrades: boolean;
  wantsAccount: boolean;
  lastPongAt: number;
};

const DB_FILE = "trading_app.db";

function openDb() {
  return new Database(DB_FILE);
}

function ensureEventsTable() {
  const db = openDb();
  try {
    db.exec(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS realtime_events (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        user_id INTEGER NULL,
        topic TEXT NOT NULL,
        payload_json TEXT NOT NULL,
        created_at INTEGER NOT NULL
      );
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_realtime_events_user_id_id ON realtime_events(user_id, id);
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_realtime_events_id ON realtime_events(id);
    `);
  } finally {
    db.close();
  }
}

function getCursor(): number {
  const db = openDb();
  try {
    const row = db.prepare(`SELECT IFNULL(MAX(id), 0) AS maxId FROM realtime_events`).get();
    return Number(row?.maxId ?? 0);
  } finally {
    db.close();
  }
}

function persistEvent(userId: number | null, topic: string, payload: any): number {
  const db = openDb();
  try {
    const stmt = db.prepare(
      `INSERT INTO realtime_events (user_id, topic, payload_json, created_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)`
    );
    const now = Date.now();
    const info = stmt.run(userId ?? null, topic, JSON.stringify(payload), now);
    return Number(info.lastInsertRowid);
  } finally {
    db.close();
  }
}

function fetchSince(userId: number | null, cursor: number, limit: number) {
  const db = openDb();
  try {
    const lim = Math.max(1, Math.min(5000, Number(limit || 500)));
    const cur = Math.max(0, Number(cursor || 0));

    // If userId present: return (global + user) events.
    // If not: return only global events.
    const rows =
      userId != null
        ? db
            .prepare(
              `
            SELECT id, user_id, topic, payload_json, created_at
            FROM realtime_events
            WHERE id > ? AND (user_id IS NULL OR user_id = ?)
            ORDER BY id ASC
            LIMIT ?
          `
            )
            .all(cur, userId, lim)
        : db
            .prepare(
              `
            SELECT id, user_id, topic, payload_json, created_at
            FROM realtime_events
            WHERE id > ? AND user_id IS NULL
            ORDER BY id ASC
            LIMIT ?
          `
            )
            .all(cur, lim);

    const events = rows.map((r: any) => {
      const payload = JSON.parse(r.payload_json);
      // Attach cursor to payload if not already there
      if (payload && typeof payload === "object" && payload.cursor == null) payload.cursor = r.id;
      return payload;
    });

    const nextCursor = rows.length ? Number(rows[rows.length - 1].id) : cur;
    return { events, nextCursor };
  } finally {
    db.close();
  }
}

function safeSend(ws: any, obj: any) {
  try {
    if (ws.readyState === ws.OPEN) ws.send(JSON.stringify(obj));
  } catch {}
}

let wss: WebSocketServer | null = null;
let sessionMw: any = null;

const stateBySocket = new WeakMap<any, ClientState>();

// Throttles (avoid spamming account snapshots every quote tick)
const lastAccountSentAt = new Map<number, number>();
const ACCOUNT_THROTTLE_MS = 900;

let quotesRev = 0;

function forEachClient(fn: (ws: any, st: ClientState) => void) {
  if (!wss) return;
  wss.clients.forEach((ws: any) => {
    const st = stateBySocket.get(ws);
    if (!st) return;
    fn(ws, st);
  });
}

function parseAndApplySession(req: any, cb: (userId: number | null) => void) {
  if (!sessionMw) return cb(null);

  // Minimal response object for session middleware
  const res = {
    getHeader() {},
    setHeader() {},
    end() {},
  };

  sessionMw(req, res, () => {
    const sid = req?.session?.userId;
    cb(typeof sid === "number" ? sid : null);
  });
}

export const liveUpdates = {
  ensure() {
    ensureEventsTable();
  },

  getCursor() {
    ensureEventsTable();
    return getCursor();
  },

  fetchSince(userId: number | null, cursor: number, limit: number) {
    ensureEventsTable();
    return fetchSince(userId, cursor, limit);
  },

  attach(params: { wss: WebSocketServer; sessionMiddleware: ReturnType<typeof session> }) {
    ensureEventsTable();
    wss = params.wss;
    sessionMw = params.sessionMiddleware;

    wss.on("connection", (ws: any, req: any) => {
      parseAndApplySession(req, (userId) => {
        const st: ClientState = {
          userId,
          quotesAll: true, // default: ALL quotes (safe for now; native can narrow later)
          quoteSymbols: new Set<string>(),
          wantsTrades: true,
          wantsAccount: true,
          lastPongAt: Date.now(),
        };

        stateBySocket.set(ws, st);

        safeSend(ws, {
          type: "hello",
          serverTime: Date.now(),
          cursor: getCursor(),
          userId: st.userId,
        });
      });

      ws.on("message", (buf: any) => {
        let msg: any;
        try {
          msg = JSON.parse(buf.toString());
        } catch {
          return;
        }

        const st = stateBySocket.get(ws);
        if (!st) return;

        if (msg?.type === "ping") {
          st.lastPongAt = Date.now();
          safeSend(ws, { type: "pong", ts: Date.now() });
          return;
        }

        // Backwards compatible "auth" (older client sends userId)
        // Security: we DO NOT trust userId if session already present.
        if (msg?.type === "auth") {
          if (st.userId == null && typeof msg.userId === "number") {
            // keep quotes-only if unauth; do not allow user topics without session
            st.userId = null;
          }
          return;
        }

        if (msg?.type === "subscribe") {
          if (msg.quotes === "ALL") {
            st.quotesAll = true;
            st.quoteSymbols.clear();
          } else if (Array.isArray(msg.quotes)) {
            st.quotesAll = false;
            msg.quotes.forEach((s: any) => st.quoteSymbols.add(String(s).toUpperCase()));
          }

          if (typeof msg.trades === "boolean") st.wantsTrades = msg.trades;
          if (typeof msg.account === "boolean") st.wantsAccount = msg.account;
          return;
        }

        if (msg?.type === "unsubscribe") {
          if (msg.quotes === "ALL") {
            st.quotesAll = false;
            st.quoteSymbols.clear();
          } else if (Array.isArray(msg.quotes)) {
            msg.quotes.forEach((s: any) => st.quoteSymbols.delete(String(s).toUpperCase()));
          }
          if (typeof msg.trades === "boolean") st.wantsTrades = !msg.trades ? false : st.wantsTrades;
          if (typeof msg.account === "boolean") st.wantsAccount = !msg.account ? false : st.wantsAccount;
          return;
        }
      });

      ws.on("close", () => {
        stateBySocket.delete(ws);
      });
    });

    // Keepalive: terminate dead sockets
    const interval = setInterval(() => {
      if (!wss) return;
      const now = Date.now();
      wss.clients.forEach((ws: any) => {
        const st = stateBySocket.get(ws);
        if (!st) return;
        if (now - st.lastPongAt > 45_000) {
          try {
            ws.terminate();
          } catch {}
        } else {
          safeSend(ws, { type: "ping", ts: now });
        }
      });
    }, 15_000);

    wss.on("close", () => clearInterval(interval));
  },

  /**
   * High-frequency quote tick (WS only)
   */
  publishQuoteTicks(quotes: Array<any>) {
    if (!wss) return;
    for (const q of quotes) {
      const symbol = String(q.symbol || "").toUpperCase();
      if (!symbol) continue;

      const evt = {
        type: "quotes:tick",
        symbol,
        name: q.name ?? "",
        price: Number(q.price),
        change: q.change != null ? Number(q.change) : undefined,
        time: q.time != null ? Number(q.time) : undefined,
        bid: q.bid != null ? Number(q.bid) : undefined,
        ask: q.ask != null ? Number(q.ask) : undefined,
        spread: q.spread != null ? Number(q.spread) : undefined,
        prevClose: q.prevClose != null ? Number(q.prevClose) : undefined,
        changePct: q.changePct != null ? Number(q.changePct) : undefined,
        percent_change: q.percent_change != null ? Number(q.percent_change) : undefined,
        isStale: !!q.isStale,
        lastApiUpdate: q.lastApiUpdate != null ? Number(q.lastApiUpdate) : undefined,
        dataAge: q.dataAge != null ? Number(q.dataAge) : undefined,
      };

      forEachClient((ws, st) => {
        if (!st.quotesAll && !st.quoteSymbols.has(symbol)) return;
        safeSend(ws, evt);
      });
    }
  },

  /**
   * Low-frequency marker: safe to persist + triggers "refetch if needed" clients
   */
  publishQuotesUpdated(symbols: string[]) {
    ensureEventsTable();
    quotesRev += 1;

    const payload = {
      type: "quotes:updated",
      rev: quotesRev,
      symbols: symbols?.map((s) => String(s).toUpperCase()).slice(0, 500) ?? [],
      ts: Date.now(),
    };

    const cursor = persistEvent(null, "quotes", payload);
    payload.cursor = cursor;

    forEachClient((ws, st) => {
      // global event
      safeSend(ws, payload);
    });
  },

  publishTradesUpdated(userId: number) {
    ensureEventsTable();
    const payload = { type: "trades:updated", userId, ts: Date.now() };
    const cursor = persistEvent(userId, "trades", payload);
    payload.cursor = cursor;

    forEachClient((ws, st) => {
      if (st.userId !== userId) return;
      if (!st.wantsTrades) return;
      safeSend(ws, payload);
    });
  },

  publishAccountUpdated(userId: number, metrics: any) {
    ensureEventsTable();

    // Throttle per user
    const last = lastAccountSentAt.get(userId) ?? 0;
    const now = Date.now();
    if (now - last < ACCOUNT_THROTTLE_MS) return;
    lastAccountSentAt.set(userId, now);

    const payload = {
      type: "account:updated",
      userId,
      balance: Number(metrics.balance),
      equity: Number(metrics.equity),
      floatingPnl: Number(metrics.floatingPnl),
      usedMargin: Number(metrics.usedMargin),
      freeMargin: Number(metrics.freeMargin),
      marginLevel: metrics.marginLevel === null ? null : Number(metrics.marginLevel),
      openPositions: Number(metrics.openPositions),
      pricingStale: !!metrics.pricingStale,
      staleSymbols: Array.isArray(metrics.staleSymbols) ? metrics.staleSymbols : [],
      asOf: metrics.asOf instanceof Date ? metrics.asOf.toISOString() : String(metrics.asOf),
    };

    const cursor = persistEvent(userId, "account", payload);
    payload.cursor = cursor;

    forEachClient((ws, st) => {
      if (st.userId !== userId) return;
      if (!st.wantsAccount) return;
      safeSend(ws, payload);
    });
  },
};

4) PATCH: server/db/ensureSchema.ts (add ensureRealtimeEventsTable)
Append this near the bottom (and export it).

// server/db/ensureSchema.ts
import Database from "better-sqlite3";

export async function ensureRealtimeEventsTable() {
  const sqlite = new Database("trading_app.db");
  try {
    sqlite.exec(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS realtime_events (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        user_id INTEGER NULL,
        topic TEXT NOT NULL,
        payload_json TEXT NOT NULL,
        created_at INTEGER NOT NULL
      );
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_realtime_events_user_id_id ON realtime_events(user_id, id);
      CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_realtime_events_id ON realtime_events(id);
    `);
  } finally {
    sqlite.close();
  }

9) NEW: client/src/live/LiveUpdatesProvider.tsx
This provider:

owns one WebSocket

auto-reconnects

updates React Query cache on quotes:tick and account:updated

invalidates trades on trades:updated

exposes a lightweight subscribe API so existing hooks can keep working

// client/src/live/LiveUpdatesProvider.tsx
import React, { createContext, useContext, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { queryClient } from "@/lib/queryClient";

type Listener = (evt: any) => void;

type LiveCtx = {
  isConnected: boolean;
  send: (msg: any) => boolean;
  addListener: (fn: Listener) => () => void;
};

const Ctx = createContext<LiveCtx | null>(null);

function getWsUrl(): string {
  const envUrl = (import.meta as any).env?.VITE_WS_BASE_URL as string | undefined;
  if (envUrl) return envUrl.replace(/\/$/, "") + "/ws";
  const proto = window.location.protocol === "https:" ? "wss:" : "ws:";
  return `${proto}//${window.location.host}/ws`;
}

function patchQuotesCache(tick: any) {
  const symbol = String(tick.symbol || "").toUpperCase();
  if (!symbol) return;

  const key = ["/api/quotes/latest"];
  queryClient.setQueryData(key, (prev: any) => {
    if (!Array.isArray(prev)) return prev;

    let found = false;
    const next = prev.map((q: any) => {
      if (String(q.symbol || "").toUpperCase() !== symbol) return q;
      found = true;
      return {
        ...q,
        ...tick,
        // keep existing fields if tick omitted them
        name: tick.name ?? q.name,
        price: tick.price ?? q.price,
        bid: tick.bid ?? q.bid,
        ask: tick.ask ?? q.ask,
        spread: tick.spread ?? q.spread,
        isStale: tick.isStale ?? q.isStale,
        lastApiUpdate: tick.lastApiUpdate ?? q.lastApiUpdate,
        dataAge: tick.dataAge ?? q.dataAge,
      };
    });

    // If not present yet, optionally append (rare)
    if (!found) next.push(tick);

    return next;
  });
}

function patchAccountCaches(evt: any) {
  // account summary cache
  queryClient.setQueryData(["/api/account/summary"], (prev: any) => {
    // If not loaded yet, seed it
    const next = {
      balance: evt.balance,
      equity: evt.equity,
      floatingPnl: evt.floatingPnl,
      usedMargin: evt.usedMargin,
      freeMargin: evt.freeMargin,
      marginLevel: evt.marginLevel,
      openPositions: evt.openPositions,
      pricingStale: evt.pricingStale,
      staleSymbols: evt.staleSymbols,
      asOf: evt.asOf,
    };
    return prev ? { ...prev, ...next } : next;
  });

  // current user cache (merge metrics only if present)
  queryClient.setQueryData(["/api/auth/current-user"], (prev: any) => {
    if (!prev) return prev;
    return {
      ...prev,
      balance: String(evt.balance),
      equity: String(evt.equity),
      freeMargin: String(evt.freeMargin),
      usedMargin: String(evt.usedMargin),
    };
  });
}

export function LiveUpdatesProvider({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  const [isConnected, setIsConnected] = useState(false);
  const wsRef = useRef<WebSocket | null>(null);
  const listenersRef = useRef(new Set<Listener>());
  const reconnectRef = useRef({ attempts: 0, timer: 0 as any });

  const connect = () => {
    const url = getWsUrl();
    const ws = new WebSocket(url);
    wsRef.current = ws;

    ws.onopen = () => {
      setIsConnected(true);
      reconnectRef.current.attempts = 0;

      // Subscribe to everything needed (server will ignore user topics if not authed)
      ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: "subscribe", quotes: "ALL", account: true, trades: true }));
      ws.send(JSON.stringify({ type: "ping" }));
    };

    ws.onclose = () => {
      setIsConnected(false);
      scheduleReconnect();
    };

    ws.onerror = () => {
      // onclose will handle reconnect
    };

    ws.onmessage = (e) => {
      let evt: any;
      try {
        evt = JSON.parse(e.data);
      } catch {
        return;
      }

      // Central cache wiring
      if (evt?.type === "quotes:tick") patchQuotesCache(evt);
      if (evt?.type === "account:updated") patchAccountCaches(evt);
      if (evt?.type === "trades:updated") {
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["/api/trades"] });
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["/api/trades/open"] });
      }

      // Fan-out to hook listeners (backwards compatible with existing hooks)
      listenersRef.current.forEach((fn) => {
        try {
          fn(evt);
        } catch {}
      });
    };
  };

  const scheduleReconnect = () => {
    const r = reconnectRef.current;
    if (r.timer) return;

    const attempt = Math.min(10, (r.attempts || 0) + 1);
    r.attempts = attempt;

    const delay = Math.min(15_000, 800 * attempt + Math.floor(Math.random() * 400));
    r.timer = window.setTimeout(() => {
      r.timer = 0;
      connect();
    }, delay);
  };

  useEffect(() => {
    connect();
    return () => {
      try {
        wsRef.current?.close();
      } catch {}
      wsRef.current = null;
    };
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, []);

  const ctx = useMemo<LiveCtx>(() => {
    return {
      isConnected,
      send: (msg: any) => {
        const ws = wsRef.current;
        if (!ws || ws.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return false;
        ws.send(JSON.stringify(msg));
        return true;
      },
      addListener: (fn: Listener) => {
        listenersRef.current.add(fn);
        return () => listenersRef.current.delete(fn);
      },
    };
  }, [isConnected]);

  return <Ctx.Provider value={ctx}>{children}</Ctx.Provider>;
}

export function useLiveUpdates() {
  const v = useContext(Ctx);
  if (!v) throw new Error("useLiveUpdates must be used within LiveUpdatesProvider");
  return v;
}
10) REPLACE: client/src/hooks/use-websocket.tsx (so all hooks share ONE connection)
This keeps the same API your existing hooks use.

// client/src/hooks/use-websocket.tsx
import { useEffect, useState, useCallback } from "react";
import { useLiveUpdates } from "@/live/LiveUpdatesProvider";

interface UseWebSocketOptions {
  onMessage?: (data: any) => void;
  onOpen?: () => void;
  onClose?: () => void;
  onError?: (error: Event) => void;
}

export function useWebSocket(_url: string, options: UseWebSocketOptions = {}) {
  const live = useLiveUpdates();
  const [wasConnected, setWasConnected] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    const unsub = live.addListener((evt) => {
      options.onMessage?.(evt);
    });
    return unsub;
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [live]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (live.isConnected && !wasConnected) {
      setWasConnected(true);
      options.onOpen?.();
    }
    if (!live.isConnected && wasConnected) {
      setWasConnected(false);
      options.onClose?.();
    }
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [live.isConnected]);

  const sendMessage = useCallback(
    (data: any) => {
      return live.send(data);
    },
    [live]
  );

  return {
    isConnected: live.isConnected,
    sendMessage,
    error: null as any,
  };
}

12) PATCH: client/src/lib/queryClient.ts (API base URL support for native)

// client/src/lib/queryClient.ts
import { QueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { toast } from "@/hooks/use-toast";

export const queryClient = new QueryClient({
  defaultOptions: {
    queries: {
      refetchInterval: false,
      refetchOnWindowFocus: false,
      staleTime: 1000 * 60 * 2,
      retry: false,
    },
    mutations: {
      retry: false,
    },
  },
});

function resolveApiUrl(url: string) {
  if (/^https?:\/\//i.test(url)) return url;
  const base = (import.meta as any).env?.VITE_API_BASE_URL as string | undefined;
  if (!base) return url;
  return base.replace(/\/$/, "") + url;
}

export async function apiRequest(
  method: string,
  url: string,
  data?: unknown | undefined
): Promise<Response> {
  const res = await fetch(resolveApiUrl(url), {
    method,
    headers: data ? { "Content-Type": "application/json" } : {},
    body: data ? JSON.stringify(data) : undefined,
    credentials: "include",
  });

  if (!res.ok) {
    const errorData = await res.json().catch(() => ({}));
    const error: any = new Error(errorData.message || "Request failed");
    error.status = res.status;
    error.response = errorData;
    throw error;
  }

  return res;
}

queryClient.setMutationDefaults(["/api/login"], {
  mutationFn: async (credentials: { email: string; password: string }) => {
    const res = await apiRequest("POST", "/api/login", credentials);
    return await res.json();
  },
  onError: (error: Error) => {
    toast({
      title: "Login failed",
      description: error.message,
      variant: "destructive",
    });
  },
});

import type { NextFunction, Request, Response } from "express";
import { and, eq, isNull } from "drizzle-orm";

import { db } from "@db";
import { userSessions } from "@shared/schema";
import {
  authenticateApiTokenFromRequest,
  shimSessionFromTokenAuth,

export const requireAuth = async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  // ------------------------------------------------------------
  // 1) Session auth (browser/web)
  // ------------------------------------------------------------
  if (req.session?.userId) {
    try {
      // Check if session is revoked
      const session = await db.query.userSessions.findFirst({
        where: and(
          eq(userSessions.sessionId, req.sessionID),
          isNull(userSessions.revokedAt)
        ),
      });

      if (!session) {
        req.session.destroy(() => {});
        return res.status(401).json({ message: "Session expired or revoked" });
      }

      return next();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("Session auth check failed:", err);
      return res.status(401).json({ message: "Not authenticated" });
    }
  }

  // ------------------------------------------------------------
  // 2) Bearer token auth (mobile/native)
  // ------------------------------------------------------------
  try {
    const tokenAuth = await authenticateApiTokenFromRequest(req);
    if (!tokenAuth) {
      return res.status(401).json({ message: "Not authenticated" });
    }

    // Attach auth context for downstream use (optional).
    (req as any).auth = tokenAuth;

    // Shim req.session fields for compatibility with existing handlers,
    // without persisting a new session cookie.
    shimSessionFromTokenAuth(req as any, tokenAuth);

    return next();
  } catch (err) {
    console.error("Token auth check failed:", err);
    return res.status(401).json({ message: "Not authenticated" });
  }
};

export const requireAdmin = async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  await requireAuth(req, res, () => {
    if (!(req.session as any)?.isAdmin) {
      return res.status(403).json({ message: "Admin access required" });
    }
    return next();
  });
};

  // Authentication middleware helper (session OR bearer token)
  const ensureAuth = async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    // ------------------------------------------------------------
    // 1) Session auth (browser/web)
    // ------------------------------------------------------------
    if (req.session?.userId) {
      // Check if session has been revoked
      const sessionId = req.sessionID;
      if (sessionId) {
        const sessionDb = new BetterSQLite3("./trading_app.db");
        try {
          const sessionRow = sessionDb.prepare(`
            SELECT revoked_at FROM user_sessions
            WHERE session_id = ? AND user_id = ?
          `).get(sessionId, req.session.userId) as { revoked_at: number | null } | undefined;

          if (sessionRow?.revoked_at) {
            req.session.destroy(() => {});
            return res.status(401).json({
              message: "Session has been terminated",
              code: "SESSION_REVOKED",
            });
          }

          // Touch session to update lastActiveAt (best-effort)
          try {
            touchSession(sessionId);
          } catch {
            // ignore
          }
        } finally {
          sessionDb.close();
        }
      }

      return next();
    }

    // ------------------------------------------------------------
    // 2) Bearer token auth (mobile/native)
    // ------------------------------------------------------------
    try {
      const tokenCtx = await authenticateApiTokenFromRequest(req);
      if (!tokenCtx) {
        return res.status(401).json({ message: "Not authenticated" });
      }

      (req as any).auth = tokenCtx;
      shimSessionFromTokenAuth(req as any, tokenCtx);

      return next();

      console.error("Token authentication failed:", err);
      return res.status(401).json({ message: "Not authenticated" });

Replace the entire function ensureOrderIntentAuditTable() with:

// Order Intent Audit table for capturing RECEIVED and DECISION events
export function ensureOrderIntentAuditTable() {
  const db = getDb();
  try {
    db.exec(`
      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS order_intent_audit (
        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
        correlation_id TEXT NOT NULL,
        event_at INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%s', 'now')),
        event_at_ms INTEGER,
        event_code TEXT NOT NULL,
        decision TEXT,
        reject_check TEXT,
        reject_reason TEXT,
        actor_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'USER',
        user_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
        session_id TEXT,
        ip TEXT,
        user_agent TEXT,

        -- NEW: Idempotency + outcome linkage
        client_request_id TEXT,
        result_trade_id INTEGER,

        symbol TEXT,
        side TEXT,
        order_type TEXT,
        time_in_force TEXT,
        qty_lots REAL,
        requested_price REAL,
        limit_price REAL,
        stop_price REAL,
        take_profit REAL,
        stop_loss REAL,
        quote_bid REAL,
        quote_ask REAL,
        quote_mid REAL,
        quote_ts INTEGER,
        quote_is_stale INTEGER,
        risk_limit_json TEXT,
        risk_observed_json TEXT,
        risk_snapshot_json TEXT,
        payload_json TEXT NOT NULL,
        prev_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
        event_hash TEXT NOT NULL
      );
    `);

    // Backfill columns if table already existed (safe runtime migration)
    const table = "order_intent_audit";
    const adds: Array<{ col: string; ddl: string }> = [
      { col: "client_request_id", ddl: "ALTER TABLE order_intent_audit ADD COLUMN client_request_id TEXT" },
      { col: "result_trade_id", ddl: "ALTER TABLE order_intent_audit ADD COLUMN result_trade_id INTEGER" },
    ];
    for (const a of adds) {
      if (!hasColumn(db, table, a.col)) {
        db.exec(a.ddl);
        console.log(`Added column ${a.col} to ${table}`);
      }
    }

    // Indexes
    db.exec(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_order_intent_correlation ON order_intent_audit(correlation_id);`);
    db.exec(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_order_intent_user ON order_intent_audit(user_id);`);
    db.exec(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_order_intent_event ON order_intent_audit(event_code);`);
    db.exec(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_order_intent_decision ON order_intent_audit(decision);`);
    db.exec(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_order_intent_client_request ON order_intent_audit(client_request_id);`);

    /**
     * Idempotency enforcement:
     * - Multiple events exist per order attempt (ORDER_RECEIVED, DECISION, etc.).
     * - Therefore: enforce uniqueness ONLY for ORDER_RECEIVED rows.
     * - SQLite partial index is perfect here.
     */
    db.exec(`
      CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ux_order_intent_idempotency_received
      ON order_intent_audit(user_id, client_request_id)
      WHERE client_request_id IS NOT NULL AND event_code = 'ORDER_RECEIVED';
    `);

    console.log("Order intent audit table ensured (with idempotency)");
  } finally {
    db.close();
  }
}

With this:

async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  const auditCtx = buildAuditContext(req);
  const receivedAtMs = Date.now();
  const userId = req.session.userId;

  // Accept clientRequestId from either header or body
  const clientRequestIdRaw =
    String(req.headers["x-client-request-id"] ?? req.headers["x-idempotency-key"] ?? "").trim() ||
    String((req.body as any)?.clientRequestId ?? "").trim();

  const clientRequestId =
    clientRequestIdRaw && clientRequestIdRaw.length >= 8 && clientRequestIdRaw.length <= 128
      ? clientRequestIdRaw
      : null;

  // If provided, enforce idempotency: return existing created trade instead of creating a duplicate
  if (clientRequestId) {
    try {
      const existing = await db.query.orderIntentAudit.findFirst({
        where: and(eq(orderIntentAudit.userId, userId), eq(orderIntentAudit.clientRequestId, clientRequestId)),
        orderBy: desc(orderIntentAudit.id),
      });

      if (existing) {
        const existingCorrelationId = (existing as any).correlationId;
        const existingTradeId = (existing as any).resultTradeId ?? null;

        // If we already have a trade outcome, return that same trade
        if (existingTradeId) {
          const t = await storage.getTradeById(existingTradeId);
          if (t) {
            // 200 is correct: this is a replay, not a new create
            res.setHeader("X-Idempotent-Replay", "1");
            res.setHeader("X-Correlation-Id", String(existingCorrelationId));
            return res.status(200).json(t);
          }
        }

        // Otherwise: we've seen the request, but outcome isn't recorded yet
        res.setHeader("X-Correlation-Id", String(existingCorrelationId));
        return res.status(202).json({
          status: "RECEIVED",
          correlationId: existingCorrelationId,
          clientRequestId,
          message: "Order already received; outcome pending.",
        });
      }
    } catch (e) {
      // Fail-open: do not block trading if idempotency lookup fails unexpectedly
      console.error("Idempotency lookup failed:", e);
    }
  }

  const correlationId = generateCorrelationId();
  auditCtx.correlationId = correlationId;

  try {
    // ...

How mobile should use this (minimal client rule)
Generate a UUID per “Place Order” click/tap.

Send it as either:

Header: X-Client-Request-Id: <uuid> (recommended)

OR body: { clientRequestId: "<uuid>" }

On network retry, send the same clientRequestId.

Result:

First request creates the trade and logs ORDER_RECEIVED → DECISION PASS with resultTradeId.

Retries return the same trade (HTTP 200, X-Idempotent-Replay: 1), preventing duplicates.
